# Spike 1431 transcript

Each heading gives the UTC time and the host the wrapper (`t.sh`) ran on. That host is always the laptop, `CJs-MacBook-Pro`. A command whose text starts `ssh … finnpc`, `gh auth token | ssh … finnpc` or `scp … finnpc:` runs on the Windows machine **Finn-PC10** (`finnpc`); its output under the heading is Finn-PC10's. The wrapper redacts any `gh?_` token string in the output. The token reached finnpc only on stdin.

Every command the spike ran, with its UTC time and host (`hostname -s`). Entries before the wrapper existed are backfilled from the session log with their recorded times.

```
$ gh issue view 1431 -R williacj/rigger --json body -q .body | shasum | cut -c1-12; gh issue view 1431 -R williacj/rigger --json body -q .body | sed -n '/Hosts, and whose/,/Rules on how/p'
3c509245f0e7
**Hosts, and whose custody each is under.**

- **The WSL2 machine and the Windows machine are the primary hosts.** Heavy or long runs go there. The spike reaches each over SSH from the laptop ([relay](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1429#issuecomment-7014074437), answer 4), at these hosts:
  - the WSL2 machine: `wsl-finnpc` (`cj@10.0.0.29`, port 2222, a Windows port proxy into WSL's sshd; until that sshd answers, WSL is reached through the Windows host with `ssh finnpc wsl.exe -e …`);
  - the Windows machine: `finnpc` (`cj@10.0.0.29`, port 2223, Windows OpenSSH).
- **The laptop** (macOS) is enrolled under M8's custody rule ([M8-U45](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1293#issuecomment-6980322109), [M8-U58](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1293#issuecomment-6988027619)). It takes light macOS runs only, and none of these:
  - reading, writing or running anything in `~/GitHub/emend-engine`, `~/GitHub/emend-engine-worktrees`, `~/GitHub/emend` or `~/rigger-builds/`, on Emend's board (project 13), or in the williacj/emend repository;
  - a LaunchAgent with the `com.williacj.rigger` prefix, or anything in `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.williacj.rigger*.plist` or `~/Library/Logs/rigger/`;
  - a Rigger run from a checkout, or one against Emend (`R-SAFE-5`);
  - a git GUI, or an IDE that fetches by itself;
  - a long CPU-heavy job during an M8 canary or window period, which rigger-ae announces.
- **The Mac mini** is under the M8 coordinator's custody and is offline. Before any use, the spike tells rigger-ae on this card and waits for rigger-ae to relay the M8 coordinator's OK. Every command there runs through `~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/coordinator-tools/suite-slot.sh <tag> -- …`. The spike never touches the `judge-*` or `codex-*` clones there.

**Rules on how it runs.**
[exit 0]

$ cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1431-spike-engineer && sed -n '380,402p' docs/v0-build-plan.md
- A missed trigger fires once on restart (`R-SCHED-8`).
- A card re-dispatched after an engine death is recorded as a redo, and `report` counts it apart
  from first-attempt work (D1) (`R-STATE-3`).
- `report` on this repository's own event stream shows the signals of every layer that has
  landed (`R-RECORD-2`, `R-RECORD-4`).
- Telemetry push comes from config: off by default writes nothing to the data ref, and on writes
  the stream there (`R-SAFE-3`).

**M8. Acceptance on Emend.**

In order:

1. Three concurrent cold `cargo build --workspace` dispatches in three real worktrees return 0,
   with survivors logged.
2. SIGKILL of Rigger during three live Engineers leaves no descendant within ten seconds, and the
   restart re-dispatches all three.
3. A bounded canary of ten Emend cards, including one requirements proposal judged by Emend's
   panel and then the owner. That panel needs two Emend assets that do not exist yet,
   a PM reviewer role and an architecture-review skill; both are Emend cards and both land first.
4. A thirty-card window with zero infrastructure-caused escalations, and every merge carrying a
   fresh verdict from every configured judge. Judged from `report`, not by hand.
5. Telemetry push is on for the whole window, and the pushed stream reads back complete
   afterwards. M9 and M10 have no other source of data.
[exit 0]

## Commands run outside the wrapper, verbatim

This section replaces the summary table that stood here at 8fb675a7. It gives each command the spike ran on the laptop without `t.sh`, verbatim, in order. Where a command wrote a file with a heredoc, the file's body follows it.

**What the times are.** A time marked **mtime** is the file's last-modification time on the laptop, read with `TZ=UTC stat -f %Sm`. It is a measurement of the write, unless a later patch, named beside it, moved it. A time marked **between** is a bracket: the wrapped transcript entries immediately before and after the command. Commands of that kind recorded no time of their own, and no exact time can be recovered for them.

**What the bodies are.** Each body below is the file as it stood at the end of the spike. Where a later patch changed a file, the heredoc's original body is the final body with that patch reverted. Every patch is quoted verbatim in "Patches". `$S` is `/Users/cjwilliams/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1431-spike-engineer/spikes/1431-spike-engineer`.

**Writes into committed files.** Where a command wrote into a file this pull request commits (the report, the journal entry, `doc-references.json`, or the evidence files), that file at the commit stands in for the body.

**The throwaway code is quoted, not committed as code.** It is in this file only as part of the record the card's transcript rule asks for. It is not runnable from the repository, and nothing in the pull request imports or executes it.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ABOUTME: Transcript wrapper for spike 1431: logs UTC time, host and command, runs it, logs its output.
T="$(dirname "$0")/transcript.md"
ts=$(date -u +%FT%TZ); h=$(hostname -s)
printf '\n### %s `%s`\n\n```\n$ %s\n' "$ts" "$h" "$*" >> "$T"
out=$(bash -c "$*" 2>&1); rc=$?
printf '%s\n' "$out" | sed -E 's/(gh[opsu]_)[A-Za-z0-9]+/\1REDACTED/g' >> "$T"
printf '[exit %s]\n```\n' "$rc" >> "$T"
printf '%s\n' "$out"; exit $rc
EOF

cat > board.js <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Board reads and writes for spike 1431's scratch Projects v2 board: read one item's
// fields, set Status and Escalation, and read every item in one paginated query.
const fs = require('fs');
const path = require('path');
const { graphql } = require('./forge');
const BOARD_FILE = process.env.BOARD_FILE || path.join(__dirname, 'board.json');
const board = () => JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(BOARD_FILE, 'utf8'));

const READ_ITEM = `query ReadItem($id: ID!) {
  node(id: $id) { ... on ProjectV2Item {
    status: fieldValueByName(name: "Status") { ... on ProjectV2ItemFieldSingleSelectValue { name } }
    escalation: fieldValueByName(name: "Escalation") { ... on ProjectV2ItemFieldTextValue { text } }
  } }
  rateLimit { cost remaining used }
}`;

async function readItem(card, tag) {
  const b = board();
  const d = await graphql('ReadItem', READ_ITEM, { id: b.items[card] }, { card, ...tag });
  return { column: d.node?.status?.name ?? null, escalation: d.node?.escalation?.text ?? '' };
}

const SET_STATUS = `mutation SetStatus($p: ID!, $i: ID!, $f: ID!, $o: String!) {
  updateProjectV2ItemFieldValue(input: { projectId: $p, itemId: $i, fieldId: $f,
    value: { singleSelectOptionId: $o } }) { projectV2Item { id } } }`;
const SET_TEXT = `mutation SetEscalation($p: ID!, $i: ID!, $f: ID!, $t: String!) {
  updateProjectV2ItemFieldValue(input: { projectId: $p, itemId: $i, fieldId: $f,
    value: { text: $t } }) { projectV2Item { id } } }`;
const CLEAR = `mutation ClearEscalation($p: ID!, $i: ID!, $f: ID!) {
  clearProjectV2ItemFieldValue(input: { projectId: $p, itemId: $i, fieldId: $f }) { projectV2Item { id } } }`;

async function setColumn(card, column, tag) {
  const b = board();
  await graphql('SetStatus', SET_STATUS, { p: b.projectId, i: b.items[card], f: b.statusFieldId,
    o: b.statusOptions[column] }, { card, column, ...tag });
}

async function setEscalation(card, text, tag) {
  const b = board();
  if (text) await graphql('SetEscalation', SET_TEXT, { p: b.projectId, i: b.items[card], f: b.escalationFieldId, t: text }, { card, ...tag });
  else await graphql('ClearEscalation', CLEAR, { p: b.projectId, i: b.items[card], f: b.escalationFieldId }, { card, ...tag });
}

const SCAN = `query ScanBoard($p: ID!, $after: String) {
  node(id: $p) { ... on ProjectV2 { items(first: 100, after: $after) {
    pageInfo { hasNextPage endCursor }
    nodes { id content { ... on Issue { number } }
      status: fieldValueByName(name: "Status") { ... on ProjectV2ItemFieldSingleSelectValue { name } }
      escalation: fieldValueByName(name: "Escalation") { ... on ProjectV2ItemFieldTextValue { text } } } } } }
  rateLimit { cost remaining used }
}`;

async function scanBoard(tag) {
  const b = board();
  const out = {};
  let after = null;
  do {
    const d = await graphql('ScanBoard', SCAN, { p: b.projectId, after }, tag);
    const items = d.node.items;
    for (const n of items.nodes) out[n.content.number] = { column: n.status?.name ?? null, escalation: n.escalation?.text ?? '' };
    after = items.pageInfo.hasNextPage ? items.pageInfo.endCursor : null;
  } while (after);
  return out;
}

module.exports = { readItem, setColumn, setEscalation, scanBoard, board };
EOF
echo ok

### mtime 2026-11-19T15:53:58Z (workflows.js, start.js, edit.js, scan.js; activities.js, worker.js and watch.js were patched later) -- laptop

cat > workflows.js <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: cardWorkflow for spike 1431: the card's truth. Holds column and escalation, advances on
// a signal, escalates on entering escalateIn and waits for an answer signal, and re-runs the
// mirror every reconcileMs while it waits, which is its drift reconcile.
const wf = require('@temporalio/workflow');

const COLUMNS = ['Ready', 'Coding', 'Review', 'Owner', 'Done'];
const { mirrorCard } = wf.proxyActivities({
  startToCloseTimeout: '2 minutes',
  heartbeatTimeout: '10 seconds',
  retry: { initialInterval: '1 second', maximumAttempts: 10 },
});
const advance = wf.defineSignal('advance');
const answer = wf.defineSignal('answer');
const stateQuery = wf.defineQuery('state');

async function cardWorkflow({ card, reconcileMs, escalateIn }) {
  let state = { column: 'Ready', escalation: '' };
  let advances = 0;
  let answered = null;
  let escalated = false;
  wf.setHandler(advance, () => { advances += 1; });
  wf.setHandler(answer, (a) => { answered = a; });
  wf.setHandler(stateQuery, () => state);
  const next = () => COLUMNS[COLUMNS.indexOf(state.column) + 1];

  await mirrorCard({ card, state, why: 'transition' });
  while (state.column !== 'Done') {
    if (state.column === escalateIn && !escalated) {
      escalated = true;
      state = { ...state, escalation: 'ambiguous: waiting on owner answer' };
      await mirrorCard({ card, state, why: 'transition' });
    }
    const waiting = state.escalation ? () => answered !== null : () => advances > 0;
    if (!(await wf.condition(waiting, reconcileMs))) {
      await mirrorCard({ card, state, why: 'reconcile' });
      continue;
    }
    if (state.escalation) answered = null; else advances -= 1;
    state = { column: next(), escalation: '' };
    await mirrorCard({ card, state, why: 'transition' });
  }
  return state;
}

module.exports = { cardWorkflow };
EOF
cat > worker.js <<'EOF'
(body: worker.js below, with the worker.js half of PATCH forge-and-worker-token-stdin reverted)
EOF
cat > start.js <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Starts one cardWorkflow per card number given, workflow id = the card.
// Usage: node start.js <reconcileMs> <escalateIn|-> <card>...
const { Client, Connection } = require('@temporalio/client');
const { board } = require('./board');
async function main() {
  const [reconcileMs, escalateIn, ...cards] = process.argv.slice(2);
  const client = new Client({ connection: await Connection.connect({ address: process.env.TEMPORAL_ADDRESS || 'localhost:7243' }) });
  for (const c of cards) {
    const id = `${board().repo}#${c}`;
    await client.workflow.start('cardWorkflow', { taskQueue: 'spike-1431', workflowId: id,
      args: [{ card: c, reconcileMs: Number(reconcileMs), escalateIn: escalateIn === '-' ? null : escalateIn }] });
    console.log(new Date().toISOString(), 'started', id);
  }
}
main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
EOF
cat > edit.js <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Plays the person: moves a card's board item to another column through the API, logged

const { setColumn } = require('./board');
const [card, column] = process.argv.slice(2);

  .then(() => console.log(new Date().toISOString(), 'edited', card, '->', column))
  .catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });

### mtime 2026-11-19T16:02:39Z (r.sh, run-plan.md) -- laptop
$ S=...; cd $S && cat > r.sh <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ABOUTME: Runs one command on the remote spike host in ~/1431-spike-engineer, with GITHUB_TOKEN
# read from stdin (never a file or argv there), logged through t.sh. Usage: HOST=<h> r.sh '<cmd>'
D="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
: "${HOST:?set HOST}"
exec "$D/t.sh" "gh auth token | ssh -o UserKnownHostsFile=$D/known_hosts $HOST 'cd ~/1431-spike-engineer && read -r GITHUB_TOKEN && export GITHUB_TOKEN TEMPORAL_ADDRESS=localhost:7243 && $1'"
EOF
chmod +x r.sh; cat > run-plan.md <<'EOF'
# Spike 1431 run plan (throwaway)

Host: the WSL2 machine over SSH (bash). The Windows machine is the fallback.
All forge calls, the server, the worker, the temporal CLI and the watchers run on that one host, so
every timestamp comes from one clock. Reconcile interval R = 30 s. Read-back delay D = 3 s.
The revert bound is 2R = 60 s: the next reconcile timer fires within R, and one mirror run takes
well under R. watch.js polls every 1 s, with a 180 s timeout.

## Deploy
1. `ssh HOST 'mkdir -p ~/1431-spike-engineer'`, then rsync the code and board.json, without node_modules or the logs.
2. Check `node -v` (needs >=20.3). If it is missing, get the Node tarball into the dir.
3. Download the Temporal CLI release into the dir. Run `./temporal --version`.
4. `npm install` (approve the core-bridge install scripts if npm 11 blocks them), then `npm ls`.
5. Start the server: `setsid nohup ./temporal server start-dev --port 7243 --ui-port 8243 --db-filename ~/1431-spike-engineer/temporal.db > server.log 2>&1 < /dev/null & echo $!` and record the pid.
6. Start the worker through r.sh: `setsid nohup node worker.js > worker.log 2>&1 < /dev/null & echo $!` and record the pid. The token is in the env, read from stdin.

## Runs
| Run | Card | Escalate in | Steps |
|---|---|---|---|
| L (lifecycle + escalation + I1 drift) | 1 | Coding | start → advance (Coding, escalates) → `temporal workflow signal --name answer` and watch for Review (time from signal) → while held in Review: edit.js 1 Done (I1), watch for Review (time from the edit) → advance ×2 → Done |
| I2a (edit between the read and the write, reconcile run) | 2 | - | held in Ready; `touch gates/2.after-read`; wait for `gates/2.after-read.held`; edit.js 2 Review; `touch gates/2.release`; readback, then the next reconcile reverts |
| I2b (same, transition run) | 2 | - | `touch gates/2.after-read`; advance → the read sees Ready and holds; edit.js 2 Done; release; the mirror writes Coding |
| I3 | 3 | - | `touch gates/3.after-writes`; advance → it writes Coding and holds; edit.js 3 Owner; release; readback; the next reconcile reverts |
| I4 | 4 | Coding | advance → Coding + escalation; `touch gates/4.after-write1`; signal answer → it writes Status=Review and holds; `kill -9 <worker pid>`; start a new worker and record its pid; a heartbeat timeout of 10 s → attempt 2 writes only Escalation |
| Twice-run | 5 | - | the reconcile runs on a card held in Ready, with no change between: the writes in the 2nd run come from calllog |
| 30-card pass | 2–32 (31 running) | - | start 5..32; once all have settled, count the mirror-read and mirror-write calls with why=reconcile in one R window; plus one `node scan.js` for the batched route |
| Event history | all | - | `temporal workflow show -w '<repo>#<n>' --output json` → evidence |

## Teardown
Kill the worker and server pids I recorded. Take a process listing. Copy back the call log, mirror-runs, histories and logs. Delete the board (deleteProjectV2) and read its node id back to show not-found. The repository is left for the owner.
EOF
echo ok

**Stated-intent addendum, spike engineer for #1431** (card revision `3c509245f0e7`). This comes before any install on a remote host.

**Host.** `ssh wsl-finnpc` reset the connection at the probe (`kex_exchange_identification: Connection reset by peer`). As rigger-ae directed, phase 2 therefore runs on the **Windows machine**, `finnpc` (Finn-PC10, Windows 10.0.26300, AMD64, Node v24.19.0, npm 11.17.0). I make no install or change in WSL.

My first intent comment already covers the following. This addendum states them exactly for Windows.
- **Directory:** everything goes in `C:\Users\CJ\1431-spike-engineer\`. Nothing goes outside it.
- **Temporal CLI:** `temporal_cli_1.10.1_windows_amd64.zip`, fetched from the temporalio/cli GitHub release with the built-in `curl.exe`. It is unpacked with `tar.exe` into that directory. It is not installed, not added to PATH, and has no installer.
- **npm install:** `@temporalio/{client,worker,workflow,activity}@1.26.0` into that directory only, using the host's existing Node. I install no Node.
- **Server:** `temporal.exe server start-dev --port 7243 --ui-port 8243 --db-filename C:\Users\CJ\1431-spike-engineer\temporal.db`. It binds localhost. I plan no firewall rule; if Windows asks for one, I decline and report it.
- **Token:** it reaches each Node process on stdin over SSH (`gh auth token | ssh finnpc "... node …"`). It is never written to a file or put on a command line there.
- **Process lifetime:** Windows OpenSSH ends a session's children when the session closes. The server and the worker therefore run inside SSH sessions that the laptop holds open in the background. Those `ssh` client processes are light, and I record each laptop pid and each Windows pid. The worker kill for row I4 is `taskkill /F /PID <recorded worker pid>`.

There is no registry, service, scheduled-task, PATH, firewall or WSL change.
EOF
$S/t.sh "gh issue comment 1431 --repo williacj/rigger --body-file $S/intent-addendum.md"   (wrapped)

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ABOUTME: Runs one cmd.exe command on the Windows spike host (finnpc) in its spike directory,
# logged through t.sh. With TOKEN=1 the gh token goes to the command's stdin. Usage: [TOKEN=1] w.sh '<cmd>'
D="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
SSH="ssh -o UserKnownHostsFile=$D/known_hosts finnpc"
if [ -n "$TOKEN" ]; then
  exec "$D/t.sh" "gh auth token | $SSH 'cd /d %USERPROFILE%\\1431-spike-engineer && $1'"
else
  exec "$D/t.sh" "$SSH 'cd /d %USERPROFILE%\\1431-spike-engineer && $1' < /dev/null"
fi

### mtime 2026-11-20T00:41:32Z (hold.sh) -- laptop
$ S=...; cd $S && cat > hold.sh <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ABOUTME: Holds one long-running cmd.exe command open on finnpc in an ssh session from the laptop,
# recording the laptop ssh pid in pids.log and the transcript. Usage: [TOKEN=1] hold.sh <name> '<cmd>'
D="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"; name=$1; cmd=$2
SSH=(ssh -o UserKnownHostsFile="$D/known_hosts" -o ServerAliveInterval=30 finnpc "cd /d %USERPROFILE%\\1431-spike-engineer && $cmd")
if [ -n "$TOKEN" ]; then gh auth token | "${SSH[@]}" & else "${SSH[@]}" < /dev/null & fi
pid=$!; ts=$(date -u +%FT%TZ)
echo "$ts laptop ssh pid=$pid holds $name: $cmd" >> "$D/pids.log"
printf '\n### %s `%s`\n\n```\n$ [held, background] %s ssh finnpc "%s"\nlaptop ssh pid=%s\n```\n' "$ts" "$(hostname -s)" "${TOKEN:+gh auth token |}" "$cmd" "$pid" >> "$D/transcript.md"
wait $pid; rc=$?
echo "$(date -u +%FT%TZ) laptop ssh pid=$pid ($name) exited rc=$rc" | tee -a "$D/pids.log" >> "$D/transcript.md"
EOF
chmod +x hold.sh; echo ok
(the three hold.sh launches log themselves as "[held, background]" entries)

### mtime 2026-11-20T00:42:43Z (sigwatch.js, watch.js) -- laptop
$ S=...; cd $S && cat > sigwatch.js <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Sends a signal through the Temporal CLI, noting the instant just before, then polls the
// card's board item every 500 ms until it shows the target column, and prints the elapsed time.
// Usage: node sigwatch.js <card> <signal> <targetColumn> [inputJson]
const { execFileSync } = require('child_process');
const { readItem, board } = require('./board');
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
async function main() {
  const [card, signal, column, input] = process.argv.slice(2);
  const args = ['workflow', 'signal', '--address', 'localhost:7243', '--workflow-id', `${board().repo}#${card}`, '--name', signal];
  if (input) args.push('--input', input);
  console.log('command: temporal.exe ' + args.map((a) => (/[\s"]/.test(a) ? `'${a}'` : a)).join(' '));
  const t0 = Date.now();
  console.log('signal sent at', new Date(t0).toISOString());
  console.log(execFileSync('temporal.exe', args, { encoding: 'utf8' }).trim());
  for (;;) {
    const at = Date.now();
    const s = await readItem(card, { purpose: 'measure' });
    if (s.column === column) { console.log(JSON.stringify({ card, column, seenAt: new Date(at).toISOString(), elapsedMs: at - t0, state: s })); return; }
    if (at - t0 > 180000) { console.log(JSON.stringify({ timedOut: true, s })); process.exitCode = 2; return; }
    await sleep(500);
  }
}
main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
EOF
(PATCH watch-interval, below)
./t.sh "scp -o UserKnownHostsFile=$S/known_hosts $S/sigwatch.js $S/watch.js finnpc:1431-spike-engineer/"   (wrapped)

### mtime 2026-11-20T00:42:59Z (editwatch.js) -- laptop
$ S=...; cd $S && cat > editwatch.js <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Plays the person: moves a card's board item to another column through the API, then
// polls every 500 ms until the board shows the workflow's column again, timing it from the edit's
// answer. Usage: node editwatch.js <card> <editColumn> <workflowColumn> [timeoutMs]
const { setColumn, readItem } = require('./board');
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
async function main() {
  const [card, editCol, wfCol, timeoutMs = '120000'] = process.argv.slice(2);
  await setColumn(card, editCol, { purpose: 'edit-drift' });
  const t0 = Date.now();
  console.log('edit answered at', new Date(t0).toISOString(), card, '->', editCol);
  for (;;) {
    const at = Date.now();
    const s = await readItem(card, { purpose: 'measure' });
    if (s.column === wfCol) { console.log(JSON.stringify({ card, revertedTo: wfCol, seenAt: new Date(at).toISOString(), elapsedMs: at - t0, state: s })); return; }
    if (at - t0 > Number(timeoutMs)) { console.log(JSON.stringify({ card, timedOut: true, boundMs: Number(timeoutMs), lastState: s })); process.exitCode = 2; return; }
    await sleep(500);
  }
}
main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
EOF
./t.sh "scp -q ... editwatch.js finnpc:1431-spike-engineer/"   (wrapped)

### mtime 2026-11-20T00:43:47Z (gated.js) -- laptop
$ S=...; cd $S && cat > gated.js <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Places a person's edit at a gate inside one mirrorCard run: arms gates/<card>.<point>,
// optionally sends an advance signal, waits until the run holds at the gate, edits the column
// through the API, then releases the run. Usage: node gated.js <card> <point> <editColumn> [advance]
const fs = require('fs');
const path = require('path');
const { execFileSync } = require('child_process');
const { setColumn, board } = require('./board');
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
const say = (...a) => console.log(new Date().toISOString(), ...a);
async function main() {
  const [card, point, editCol, adv] = process.argv.slice(2);
  const G = path.join(__dirname, 'gates');
  fs.mkdirSync(G, { recursive: true });
  fs.writeFileSync(path.join(G, `${card}.${point}`), '');
  say('armed', `gates/${card}.${point}`);
  if (adv) { execFileSync('temporal.exe', ['workflow', 'signal', '--address', 'localhost:7243', '--workflow-id', `${board().repo}#${card}`, '--name', 'advance']); say('sent advance'); }
  while (!fs.existsSync(path.join(G, `${card}.${point}.held`))) await sleep(100);
  say('run holds at', point);
  if (editCol !== '-') { await setColumn(card, editCol, { purpose: 'edit-drift' }); say('edit answered', card, '->', editCol); }
  fs.writeFileSync(path.join(G, `${card}.release`), '');
  say('released');
}
main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
EOF
./t.sh "scp -q ... gated.js finnpc:1431-spike-engineer/"   (wrapped)

// ABOUTME: Deletes spike 1431's scratch board with deleteProjectV2, then reads its node id and its
// number back and prints the forge's answers.
const { graphql } = require('./forge');
const b = require('./board.json');
async function main() {
  const d = await graphql('DeleteProject', `mutation DeleteProject($p: ID!) { deleteProjectV2(input: { projectId: $p }) { projectV2 { id number } } }`, { p: b.projectId }, { purpose: 'teardown' });
  console.log('deleted', JSON.stringify(d));
  for (const [op, q, v] of [
    ['ReadDeletedNode', 'query ReadDeletedNode($p: ID!) { node(id: $p) { id } rateLimit { cost } }', { p: b.projectId }],
    ['ReadDeletedNumber', 'query ReadDeletedNumber($n: Int!) { user(login: "williacj") { projectV2(number: $n) { id } } rateLimit { cost } }', { n: b.projectNumber }],
  ]) {
    const res = await fetch('https://api.github.com/graphql', { method: 'POST', headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${process.env.GITHUB_TOKEN}`, 'content-type': 'application/json', 'user-agent': 'spike-1431' }, body: JSON.stringify({ query: q, variables: v }) });
    const text = await res.text();
    require('./forge').log({ ts: new Date().toISOString(), api: 'graphql', rw: 'read', method: 'POST', operation: op, status: res.status, purpose: 'teardown' });
    console.log(op, res.status, text);
  }
}
main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
EOF

## Final file bodies for the patched files

### forge.js (final)
```
// ABOUTME: Forge client for spike 1431: every REST and GraphQL call goes through here and is
// appended to calllog.jsonl with its time, method, endpoint or operation, and rate-limit answers.
const fs = require('fs');
const path = require('path');
const LOG = process.env.CALL_LOG || path.join(__dirname, 'calllog.jsonl');
// Unset GITHUB_TOKEN: read it from stdin's first line, so on the remote host it never lands in a
// file or a command line.
const TOKEN = () => {
  if (!process.env.GITHUB_TOKEN) process.env.GITHUB_TOKEN = fs.readFileSync(0, 'utf8').split(/\r?\n/)[0].trim();
  const t = process.env.GITHUB_TOKEN;
  if (!t) throw new Error('GITHUB_TOKEN unset');
  return t;
};

function rl(h) {
  return {
    resource: h.get('x-ratelimit-resource'), limit: h.get('x-ratelimit-limit'),
    remaining: h.get('x-ratelimit-remaining'), used: h.get('x-ratelimit-used'),
    reset: h.get('x-ratelimit-reset'),
  };
}

function log(entry) { fs.appendFileSync(LOG, JSON.stringify(entry) + '\n'); }

// A call that fails below HTTP (DNS, TLS, reset) is still a call that may have landed: log it.
async function guarded(send, entry) {
  try { return await send(); } catch (e) {
    log({ ...entry, status: 'network-error', error: String(e.cause?.code || e.message) });
    throw e;
  }
}

// tag: { purpose, card } — purpose separates production calls (setup, mirror-read, mirror-write)
// from instrumentation (readback, measure, edit-drift).
async function rest(method, endpoint, body, tag = {}) {
  const t0 = new Date();
  const res = await guarded(() => fetch('https://api.github.com' + endpoint, {
    method,
    headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${TOKEN()}`, accept: 'application/vnd.github+json',
      'x-github-api-version': '2022-11-28', 'user-agent': 'spike-1431' },
    body: body ? JSON.stringify(body) : undefined,
  }), { ts: t0.toISOString(), api: 'rest', rw: method === 'GET' ? 'read' : 'write', method, endpoint, ...tag });
  const text = await res.text();
  log({ ts: t0.toISOString(), ms: Date.now() - t0, api: 'rest', rw: method === 'GET' ? 'read' : 'write',
    method, endpoint, status: res.status, ratelimit: rl(res.headers), ...tag });
  if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`${method} ${endpoint} ${res.status}: ${text}`);
  return text ? JSON.parse(text) : null;
}

// op: the GraphQL operation name; rw: 'read' for queries, 'write' for mutations.
async function graphql(op, query, variables, tag = {}) {
  const t0 = new Date();
  const res = await guarded(() => fetch('https://api.github.com/graphql', {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${TOKEN()}`, 'user-agent': 'spike-1431',
      'content-type': 'application/json' },
    body: JSON.stringify({ query, variables }),
  }), { ts: t0.toISOString(), api: 'graphql', rw: /^\s*mutation/.test(query) ? 'write' : 'read', method: 'POST', operation: op, ...tag });
  const json = await res.json();
  const rw = /^\s*mutation/.test(query) ? 'write' : 'read';
  log({ ts: t0.toISOString(), ms: Date.now() - t0, api: 'graphql', rw, method: 'POST', operation: op,
    status: res.status, ratelimit: rl(res.headers), cost: json?.data?.rateLimit?.cost ?? null,
    errors: json.errors ? json.errors.map((e) => e.type || e.message) : undefined, ...tag });
  if (!res.ok || json.errors) throw new Error(`${op}: ${res.status} ${JSON.stringify(json.errors)}`);
  return json.data;
}

module.exports = { rest, graphql, log };
```

### activities.js (final)
```
// ABOUTME: mirrorCard activity for spike 1431: reads the card's board item, writes each field that
// differs from the workflow's full state, waits, reads back, and logs the run. File gates let the
// spike place an edit or a kill at a chosen point inside one run.
const fs = require('fs');
const path = require('path');
const { Context } = require('@temporalio/activity');
const { readItem, setColumn, setEscalation } = require('./board');

const DIR = __dirname;
const GATES = process.env.GATE_DIR || path.join(DIR, 'gates');
const RUNS = path.join(DIR, 'mirror-runs.jsonl');
const READBACK_DELAY_MS = Number(process.env.READBACK_DELAY_MS || 3000);
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
const now = () => new Date().toISOString();
const note = (e) => {
  fs.appendFileSync(RUNS, JSON.stringify({ ts: now(), ...e }) + '\n');
  try { Context.current().heartbeat(e.event); } catch { /* outside an activity */ }
};

// If gates/<card>.<point> exists: rename it to .<point>.held, then wait (heartbeating) until
// gates/<card>.release exists, consume it, and go on. One-shot per arming.
async function gate(card, point) {
  const armed = path.join(GATES, `${card}.${point}`);
  if (!fs.existsSync(armed)) return;
  fs.renameSync(armed, `${armed}.held`);
  note({ card, event: 'gate-held', point, pid: process.pid });
  const release = path.join(GATES, `${card}.release`);
  while (!fs.existsSync(release)) { Context.current().heartbeat(point); await sleep(250); }
  fs.rmSync(release);
  fs.rmSync(`${armed}.held`, { force: true });
  note({ card, event: 'gate-released', point });
}

async function mirrorCard({ card, state, why }) {
  const info = Context.current().info;
  const tag = { attempt: info.attempt, why };
  note({ card, event: 'start', why, attempt: info.attempt, workflowState: state, pid: process.pid });
  const before = await readItem(card, { purpose: 'mirror-read', ...tag });
  note({ card, event: 'read', before });
  await gate(card, 'after-read');
  const writes = [];
  if (before.column !== state.column) {
    await setColumn(card, state.column, { purpose: 'mirror-write', ...tag });
    writes.push('Status');
    note({ card, event: 'wrote', field: 'Status', value: state.column });
    await gate(card, 'after-write1');
  }
  if ((before.escalation || '') !== (state.escalation || '')) {
    await setEscalation(card, state.escalation, { purpose: 'mirror-write', ...tag });
    writes.push('Escalation');
    note({ card, event: 'wrote', field: 'Escalation', value: state.escalation });
  }
  await gate(card, 'after-writes');
  for (let w = 0; w < READBACK_DELAY_MS; w += 1000) { Context.current().heartbeat('readback-wait'); await sleep(Math.min(1000, READBACK_DELAY_MS - w)); }
  const after = await readItem(card, { purpose: 'readback', ...tag });
  const match = after.column === state.column && (after.escalation || '') === (state.escalation || '');
  note({ card, event: 'end', why, attempt: info.attempt, writes, after, workflowState: state, match,
    readbackDelayMs: READBACK_DELAY_MS });
  return { before, writes, after, match };
}

module.exports = { mirrorCard };
```

### worker.js (final)
```
// ABOUTME: The spike's single Temporal worker. Reads GITHUB_TOKEN from stdin's first line when it
// is unset, so the token never lands in a file or a command line on the remote host.
const fs = require('fs');
const path = require('path');
const { Worker, NativeConnection } = require('@temporalio/worker');
const activities = require('./activities');

async function main() {
  if (!process.env.GITHUB_TOKEN) process.env.GITHUB_TOKEN = fs.readFileSync(0, 'utf8').split(/\r?\n/)[0].trim();
  fs.appendFileSync(path.join(__dirname, 'pids.log'), `${new Date().toISOString()} worker pid=${process.pid}\n`);
  const connection = await NativeConnection.connect({ address: process.env.TEMPORAL_ADDRESS || 'localhost:7243' });
  const worker = await Worker.create({ connection, taskQueue: 'spike-1431',
    workflowsPath: require.resolve('./workflows'), activities });
  await worker.run();
}
main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
```

### watch.js (final)
```
// ABOUTME: Polls one card's board item every intervalMs until its column equals the target, and
// prints the elapsed time from a given start instant. Usage: node watch.js <card> <column> <startIso> [intervalMs] [timeoutMs]
const { readItem } = require('./board');
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
async function main() {
  const [card, column, startIso, intervalMs = '500', timeoutMs = '180000'] = process.argv.slice(2);
  const t0 = Date.parse(startIso);
  for (;;) {
    const at = Date.now();
    const s = await readItem(card, { purpose: 'measure' });
    if (s.column === column) {
      console.log(JSON.stringify({ card, column, seenAt: new Date(at).toISOString(), elapsedMs: at - t0, state: s }));
      return;
    }
    if (at - t0 > Number(timeoutMs)) { console.log(JSON.stringify({ card, column, timedOut: true, lastState: s, elapsedMs: at - t0 })); process.exitCode = 2; return; }
    await sleep(Number(intervalMs));
  }
}
main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
```

### setup.js (final)
```
// ABOUTME: Creates spike 1431's scratch forge objects: a private user repository, a user-owned
// Projects v2 board linked to it with Status options Ready..Done and an Escalation text field,
// and N issues added to the board. Writes their ids to board.json. Usage: node setup.js <N>
const fs = require('fs');
const path = require('path');
const { rest, graphql } = require('./forge');
const OWNER = 'williacj';
const REPO = 'rigger-1431-mirror-scratch';
const COLUMNS = ['Ready', 'Coding', 'Review', 'Owner', 'Done'];
const COLORS = ['GRAY', 'BLUE', 'YELLOW', 'ORANGE', 'GREEN'];
const OUT = path.join(__dirname, 'board.json');
const tag = { purpose: 'setup' };
const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));

async function main() {
  const n = Number(process.argv[2] || 32);
  const b = fs.existsSync(OUT) ? JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(OUT, 'utf8')) : { repo: REPO, items: {} };
  const save = () => fs.writeFileSync(OUT, JSON.stringify(b, null, 2));
  if (!b.repoNodeId) {
    const r = await rest('POST', '/user/repos', { name: REPO, private: true, auto_init: true,
      description: 'Scratch repository for rigger #1431 (throwaway spike). Owner deletes by hand.' }, tag);
    Object.assign(b, { repoUrl: r.html_url, repoNodeId: r.node_id, repoPrivate: r.private }); save();
  }
  if (!b.projectId) {
    const v = await graphql('Viewer', 'query Viewer { viewer { id login } rateLimit { cost remaining used } }', {}, tag);
    const d = await graphql('CreateProject', `mutation CreateProject($o: ID!, $r: ID!) {
      createProjectV2(input: { ownerId: $o, title: "rigger-1431 mirror scratch", repositoryId: $r }) {
        projectV2 { id number url owner { __typename } } } }`, { o: v.viewer.id, r: b.repoNodeId }, tag);
    const p = d.createProjectV2.projectV2;
    Object.assign(b, { projectId: p.id, projectNumber: p.number, projectUrl: p.url, projectOwnerType: p.owner.__typename }); save();
  }
  if (!b.statusFieldId) {
    const d = await graphql('ReadFields', `query ReadFields($p: ID!) { node(id: $p) { ... on ProjectV2 {
      field(name: "Status") { ... on ProjectV2SingleSelectField { id options { id name } } } } }
      rateLimit { cost remaining used } }`, { p: b.projectId }, tag);
    const f = d.node.field;
    const u = await graphql('SetStatusOptions', `mutation SetStatusOptions($f: ID!, $o: [ProjectV2SingleSelectFieldOptionInput!]) {
      updateProjectV2Field(input: { fieldId: $f, singleSelectOptions: $o }) {
        projectV2Field { ... on ProjectV2SingleSelectField { id options { id name } } } } }`,
      { f: f.id, o: COLUMNS.map((name, i) => ({ name, color: COLORS[i], description: '' })) }, tag);
    const sf = u.updateProjectV2Field.projectV2Field;
    b.statusFieldId = sf.id;
    b.statusOptions = Object.fromEntries(sf.options.map((o) => [o.name, o.id])); save();
  }
  if (!b.escalationFieldId) {
    const d = await graphql('CreateEscalationField', `mutation CreateEscalationField($p: ID!) {
      createProjectV2Field(input: { projectId: $p, dataType: TEXT, name: "Escalation" }) {
        projectV2Field { ... on ProjectV2Field { id } } } }`, { p: b.projectId }, tag);
    b.escalationFieldId = d.createProjectV2Field.projectV2Field.id; save();
  }
  for (let i = Object.keys(b.items).length + 1; i <= n; i += 1) {
    // A run cut off after creating issue i but before adding it resumes on that issue.
    const issue = await rest('GET', `/repos/${OWNER}/${REPO}/issues/${i}`, null, tag).catch(() => null)
      || await rest('POST', `/repos/${OWNER}/${REPO}/issues`, { title: `Scratch card ${i}` }, tag);
    const d = await graphql('AddItem', `mutation AddItem($p: ID!, $c: ID!) {
      addProjectV2ItemById(input: { projectId: $p, contentId: $c }) { item { id } } }`,
      { p: b.projectId, c: issue.node_id }, tag);
    b.items[issue.number] = d.addProjectV2ItemById.item.id; save();
    await sleep(1500);
  }
  console.log(JSON.stringify({ repoUrl: b.repoUrl, repoPrivate: b.repoPrivate, projectUrl: b.projectUrl,
    projectNumber: b.projectNumber, projectOwnerType: b.projectOwnerType, items: Object.keys(b.items).length }));
}
main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
```

## Revision 1 (round-1 verdict on PR #1435), laptop `CJs-MacBook-Pro`, 2026-11-20 from about 16:10Z

The commands are listed in order. A write into a committed file has that file's diff from
8fb675a7 as its body (`git diff 8fb675a7 -- <file>`). The read-only analyses are verbatim.

```
$ gh api repos/williacj/rigger/issues/comments/7044457092 -q .body        (the verdict; saved by the session to a tool-results file)
$ cd ~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1431-spike-engineer && git fetch -q origin && git status -sb | head -2 && python3 - <<'EOF'
import json,collections,glob
ids=collections.Counter()
for f in sorted(glob.glob('docs/spikes/projects-v2-mirror/histories/*.json')):
  for e in json.load(open(f))['events']:
    for k,v in e.items():
      if isinstance(v,dict) and 'identity' in v: ids[(f[-11:],e['eventType'][11:],v['identity'])]+=1
for k,v in sorted(ids.items()): print(v,k)
EOF
$ python3 - <<'EOF'      (rate-limit buckets, in-flight concurrency, busiest 60 s windows; finnpc entries of calllog.json)
import json,collections
from datetime import datetime,timedelta
P=lambda s: datetime.fromisoformat(s.replace('Z','+00:00'))
A=json.load(open('docs/spikes/projects-v2-mirror/calllog.json'))['calls']
R=[e for e in A if e['host'].startswith('Finn')]
g=[e for e in R if e['api']=='graphql' and e.get('ratelimit') and e['ratelimit'].get('used')]
for b in sorted(set(e['ratelimit']['reset'] for e in g)):
  bb=[e for e in g if e['ratelimit']['reset']==b]
  u=[int(e['ratelimit']['used']) for e in bb]
  q=sum(e['cost'] or 0 for e in bb if e['rw']=='read'); m=sum(1 for e in bb if e['rw']=='write')
  print('bucket',b,len(bb),'reads',sum(1 for e in bb if e['rw']=='read'),'querycost',q,'mutations',m,'used min',min(u),'max',max(u),'first',u[0],'last',u[-1], bb[0]['ts'],bb[-1]['ts'])
allg=[e for e in R if e['api']=='graphql']
print('remote graphql calls',len(allg),'reads',sum(e['rw']=='read' for e in allg),'writes',sum(e['rw']=='write' for e in allg), 'querycost', sum(e['cost'] or 0 for e in allg if e['rw']=='read'))
ev=[]
for e in A:
  if 'ms' in e:
    s=P(e['ts']); ev.append((s,1)); ev.append((s+timedelta(milliseconds=e['ms']),-1))
ev.sort(); c=mx=0
for t,d in ev:
  c+=d; mx=max(mx,c)
print('max concurrent requests',mx)
ts=[(P(e['ts']),e) for e in R]; ts.sort(key=lambda x:x[0])
best={'all':0,'writes':0,'sec':0,'prodwrites':0}
for i,(t,_) in enumerate(ts):
  w=[e for t2,e in ts[i:] if t2<t+timedelta(seconds=60)]
  allc=len(w); wr=sum(e['rw']=='write' for e in w); sec=sum(5 if e['rw']=='write' else 1 for e in w if e['api']=='graphql')
  pw=sum(e['rw']=='write' and e.get('purpose')=='mirror-write' for e in w)
  for k,v in (('all',allc),('writes',wr),('sec',sec),('prodwrites',pw)):
    if v>best[k]: best[k]=v
print('peak 60s window', best)
